ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u10	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project aims to implement European Union law and concerns the implementation of Directive 2016/1629 of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union, which lays down the technical requirements necessary to ensure the safety and classification of ships operating on waterways. The Commission carried out its first reading at its meeting on 15 January this year. and immediately after the first reading, it proceeded to consider the draft law. The Commission adopted amendments of an editorial, legislative and substantive nature. The substantive amendment consisted in the introduction of a definition of a traditional watercraft. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u11	Thank you, sir. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. I invite Mr Jerzy Wilk to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. I would like to ask your Members to move the talks to the roundabouts of the plenary room. I would ask you to speak, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u12	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The draft law amending the IWT Act aims to implement two EU directives: the first of 2016 - 2016/1629, and the second of 2018 - 2018/970. Those Directives lay down the technical requirements necessary to ensure the safety of vessels sailing on inland waterways in Poland and European countries. The bill provides for the following solutions. Firstly, the possibility of issuing an EU inland navigation certificate is introduced. This document replaces the existing ship safety document and allows ships to operate on waterways in Poland and European countries. Secondly, a new document supplementing the EU inland navigation certificate is introduced. This document is intended to allow shipowners to operate on waterways for which additional technical requirements have been introduced for ships. To date, Polish shipowners could only obtain such a document in other European countries which introduced additional requirements for ships. Thirdly, the possibility of renewing security documents is introduced, i.e. legalisation of a document which has already expired. To date, in the event of the expiry of the document, the ship owner had to apply for a new document. The solution introduced will shorten and simplify this procedure. Fourthly, State supervision of the so-called activities is ensured. classification bodies, i.e. entities which perform, inter alia, technical inspection of vessels. The Minister responsible for inland waterway transport will monitor compliance by these institutions and may request the European Commission to withdraw the authorisation to carry out inspections in the event of infringements. Fifthly, the security documents shall be kept public. All interested parties will be able to view or obtain copies of such documents. Such a solution will enable persons using the vessel to verify that the ships comply with safety requirements. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law amending the IWT Act and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u13	Thank you, sir. And I invite Mr Leszek Ruszczyk, the Civic Platform Club, the Civic Coalition, to present a position on behalf of this club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform presents its position on the government draft law amending the IWT Act. This law is the implementation of a Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union laying down the technical requirements necessary for the classification of waterways and ensuring the safety of vessels sailing therein. In addition, it regulates the responsibilities of the experts and chairmen of the inspection committees. It also lays down the conditions for obtaining Union authorisations for shipowners and introduces an obligation to obtain Union inland navigation certificates. When discussing this law on inland waterway transport, it is difficult not to express any concern about the situation prevailing in the State Water Farm of Poland. For example, in the Mazowieckie voivodship, after 13 months of operation, we are saddened to see organisational chaos, lack of regulation of labour matters, lack of working regulations, lack of permanent employment contracts, unregulated property matters, non-contractual use of self-government property since July 2018, selective acquisition of water equipment, where there is no other authorized entity on the market, lack of reviews of these equipment throughout 2018. During exchange 100% executives, directors, managers have been disposed of by highly qualified professionals, people with many years of experience and experience. The directors of water catchment managements do not have elementary decision-making powers in both human and financial matters, and above all in organisational and technical matters. It's up to the HQ. Such a lack of autonomy extends or directly paralyzes decision-making processes, even when carrying out tasks related to the proper maintenance of the waters and areas surrounding them, such as mowing, shaming, sowing, elimination of animal damage such as beavers, foxes, voles and wild boars. But according to media reports, the National Water Farm of Poland has also achieved a certain success in spending PLN 19 million. Because computers were bought, unfortunately without software, and 200 SUVs. This is impressive, especially in the absence of tractors, water appliances and even shovels. With such a dramatic drain of professional personnel, the low level of employees' salaries to buy new cars for directors appointed from the party key is scandalous. The question arises: Is the principal driving a luxury business car the most important goal of the company? The thread of sailing must also not overlook the queen of the rivers of Poland - Vistula, the largest undeveloped river in Europe. It was abandoned to work on her cavitation. Because of the widespread consolidation of the belief that water transport is not attractive on the river, any activity is dying. The only work carried out on the river is related to flood safety. Only on small local routes are small shipping units. The lack of stability of the water level, i.e. the lack of parameters for swimming, causes shipping to die. In short, the river with huge economic, transport and energy potential is forgotten and neglected. Nobody even plans intermodal transport hubs including river ports. Ladies and gentlemen! We are losing the historic opportunity and opportunity to rebuild the E40 waterway linking the Baltic Sea with the Black Sea. I am appealing here to the parliamentarians from the Vistula River to ask us to invest in our river. It's not just our chance, it's our duty. In conclusion, the Civic Platform Club - the Civic Coalition is an ally of the development of inland waterway transport and sees the enormous economic potential of river shipping. Let this bill draw the attention of the government to the need to develop an infrastructure investment plan dedicated exclusively to the Vistula. On behalf of the club, I declare that we will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

We are also concerned about the amount of fines imposed on the classification institution - this is a penalty of up to 100,000. zł for failure to notify within a specified period the Minister responsible for inland navigation of any change causing failure to comply with the necessary requirements for the performance of the functions of a recognised classification body or for failure to promptly communicate any information and documents confirming non-compliance with those requirements and the continued performance of the tasks of a recognised classification body, the penalty for failure to comply with the information obligation to change the name or address of the classification body, for making it difficult or impossible for a classification body to start or carry out checks on non-compliance by it, and the penalties for ship owners for failure to provide information on ship scrapping and information on changes in the name, ship owner, registration or home port, the lack of information regarding the change in the technical parameters of the ship subject to entry in the Union inland navigation certificate, the change of which may lead to a risk of safety in inland navigation. We have questions about the influence of the above sanctions. We have no doubt that high penalties for offences that may lead to a safety risk in inland waterway transport are right, but we would like to know as a club whether there have been serious breaches of safety rules in these aspects so far and what impacts on the state budget per year we can talk about. Since the aim of the proposed law is to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 September 2016 laying down technical requirements for inland waterway vessels, which provides for the establishment of the technical requirements necessary to ensure the safety of vessels sailing on inland waterways of the European Union, we see no objections to its adoption by the Sejm. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u26	The aim of the bill under review is to implement European Union law on the implementation of the EP and EU Council Directive 2016/1629, which lays down the technical requirements necessary to ensure the safety and classification of vessels operating on waterways. The bill introduces e.g.: 1. cover the scope of the Act on the issue of Union inland navigation certificates, including certain categories of sea vessels and ships intended solely for sport or recreation; 2. replacement of Community inland navigation certificates and Community temporary inland navigation certificates by Union inland navigation certificates or Union inland navigation certificates respectively, 3. detailing and modifying the procedure for obtaining a duplicate safety document for the vessel in the event of loss or destruction of such document, 4. clarification of the provisions on the posting and making available of data in the European ship database, 5. regulation of maximum periods of validity of safety documents for certain categories of ships, 6. to allow a ship owner who is not subject to the provisions of this Directive to carry out a technical inspection and to obtain a Community inland navigation certificate if the ship complies with the requirements laid down in the Directive. Implementation of the above mentioned provisions Whereas the directive is necessary in order to ensure a correct and consistent international regulation of a harmonised system of technical requirements for and supervision of inland waterway vessels, the Kukiz Club15 therefore supports the draft law.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u30	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present our position on the draft law amending the Law on the Rules for the Management of State Property and certain other laws, prints No 3053 and 3165. I would like to inform you, Marshal, that after I speak on her behalf, I will make two amendments. I'm gonna let you tell me a little bit about them. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will make two amendments to this project. The first concerns Article. 11 point (3) (a) b. It is proposed to make a new sound. The aim of this amendment is to avoid any question of interpretation which might arise as a result of the initial wording of the proposal. As a result of the proposed amendment, the exception to the sale by PKP of fixed assets within the meaning of the Act of 29 September 1994 on Accounting by way of a tender will be to provide fixed assets to the company as a non-monetary contribution to cover the increased share capital in exchange for the inclusion of shares in that company, in particular for the implementation of the government programme. And the second amendment that Parliamentary Club Law and Justice makes to this bill is that of Article. 18 make a new sound. The purpose of this amendment is to add to the catalogue the entities which granted the power of attorney prior to the entry into force of the Act which will be left in force, including other bodies entitled under separate provisions to represent the State Treasury, as they also comply with the amended wording of the article. Article 6 (6) (a) 3 Laws on the rules for the management of State property will be able to order, in accordance with the rules and procedures laid down in separate provisions, legal or natural persons to carry out certain legal or factual or factual acts in respect of their jurisdiction by giving powers in this respect. It is therefore appropriate that other authorities authorised under separate provisions to represent the Treasury should also be included in this transitional provision. He would like to point out that this project concerns the removal of interpretation doubts which have arisen since the entry into force of the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property, the clarification of some of its provisions and the addition of new solutions to clarify the competences and tasks of government administrations. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice with these amendments, which I am submitting to your marshal, intends to support this project. Thank you very much.

We can even say that we are dealing with solutions that have not improved the system of supervision of state property, and the models introduced by this law do not withstand the test of time. In the absence of time, I will give you two examples. It is deceptive to believe that focusing all supervisory powers in the hands of the President of the Council of Ministers will make this supervision more effective, more efficient. An example of the energy sector shows that this is not the case. In fact, this law has built sectoral Poland, where each of the resorts has an idea for an area which is assigned to it, subject to a legal act. Secondly, in this law there was built a kind of one-storey supervision of state property, e.g. in the area of disposal of items at the disposal of individual state-owned legal persons. And what does this upstairs oversight of state property lead to? In fact, it leads to the situation that one is looking at the other and, as a result, it is not really clear on whose side the responsibility lies, where there is a reason for certain decisions on the management of state property. In order not to be literal, I will use an example of the doubts that have arisen with regard to the availability of space - which the Polish company made. As we have seen, the representatives of the airports say: after all, the prosecutors also took part in these decisions to dispose of the property, so it should be all right. Here we are dealing with the creation of a number of actors who are involved in the management of this state property, and consequently one is looking at the other and, in principle, the responsibility is becoming increasingly blurred. There is no justification for maintaining the Capital Investment Fund in this Act. It is a specific legal structure, which assumes that in principle two people in the state receive the right to dispose, in principle beyond any control, of PLN 2 billion per year, at least such amount results from the figures indicated in the explanatory memorandum to this law. These objectives can be pursued in a different way, controlled by the State, rather than in the way proposed in this law. At the same time, there is no justification for this fund being allocated from the Fund for the Restructuring of Entrepreneurs. Over the years, the funds from this fund have been used to help entrepreneurs, including the private sector, who are in need. Today, these loans, the aid granted to these entrepreneurs, are gradually repaid. Including interest [[♪ The bell ♪]] this represents a significant amount of money. Please note that if we give up this fund, we will at the same time deprive entrepreneurs of the opportunity to help, and at the same time, through the creation of the Capital Investment Fund we will continue the process of nationalising the Polish economy, because that is what this is going to do. I don't think that's what this is about in the democratic economy, in the market economy. As it seemed so far, the market economy is based on private property rather than on increasing the volume of state ownership. Hence our proposals for a proposed bill. Thank you very much. The aim of this proposal is to bring the system of law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 22 March 2017 declaring the non-compliance of the Article. 204 law Penal Code implementing the Constitution. The Constitutional Tribunal held that the provisions of the Enforcement Code, in so far as they do not provide for the right of personal participation of the perpetrator to whom a safeguard measure is applied consisting of the placing in a closed mental institution, in a court meeting on the further application of that measure, are incompatible with the Constitution. The ruling of the tribunal has entered into force and already persons in psychiatric institutions may demand that their right to be heard be taken into account. However, it should be noted that it is now decided by the court which issued the judgment at first instance to continue to apply the safeguard measure, which is often far from where the perpetrator is present. In order to comply with the judgment of the Tribunal, a standard should be adopted which will re-establish the jurisdiction of the court, which will rule on the further need for the perpetrator to reside in a mental institution. During his work on the project, the Ombudsman sought to allow the court to meet in a mental institution, indicating that such a solution is justified for reasons of reasonableness, purpose and cost-effectiveness in the disposal of public funds. However, this proposal did not meet with the understanding of the designers. The Ombudsman pointed out that the organisation of a police convoy for designated perpetrators to the courts could have a negative impact on the security of all citizens, as these tasks will be carried out by police officers who are currently performing other duties. It is not without significance that these convoys can be quite large given the number of people in detention and the fact that the need for further stay in the establishment is verified every 6 months. This would also be beneficial for patients whose transport involves using direct coercion measures against them. An amendment to the draft was adopted in committee, whereby, in exceptional cases, the procedural authority may decide to pay the expert before drawing up an opinion, once or in instalments, a certain amount of money for remuneration, if justified by the particular time-consuming nature of the action necessary to deliver an opinion or other specific circumstances. This amendment concerns a situation in which the time-consuming work actually results in a high level of involvement of the expert and he does not receive any fees. Therefore, this proposal will in many cases give the expert a chance to finish his work early and receive a salary before giving his opinion. High Izbo! In the course of further work on the project, consideration should be given to and examination of the possibility of introducing the possibility of court meetings at the place of the defendants' detention, i.e. in psychiatric institutions. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u94	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present to the High House the resolution adopted by the Senate at its meeting on 14 December 2018 on the submission to the Sejm of a draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure together with the draft law, print No 3134. The draft law is the implementation of the request contained in the citizens' petition submitted to the Senate of the Republic of Poland on 24 November 2017. The aim of the proposed law is to eliminate discrepancies in the way in which the calculation of procedural time limits in criminal proceedings and in civil and administrative proceedings is regulated. In the case of criminal proceedings, if the deadline for the execution of the proceedings falls on Saturday, those proceedings must be completed at the latest on that date, unless on Saturday there is one of the holidays indicated in the Act on days off from work. Then the end of the deadline for the performance of the activity will come on the next day, which is not a day off from work. On the other hand, in the case of civil and administrative proceedings, if the end of the procedural period is Saturday, that period expires on the next day, which is not a day off from work. The lack of legal harmonisation of the criminal procedure with civil and administrative treatment of Saturdays in particular procedures is not justified and requires legislative interference. The draft law that has been prepared solves this problem. The draft law provides for the introduction into the article. 123 § 3 the same solution as in civil, administrative and tax proceedings. Therefore, the draft amendment provides for a solution which will make Saturdays equal to days off from work. The last day of the deadline for Saturday will be postponed to the day following the day or days off from work. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 2 The act shall be defined as the standard 14-day period of vacathio legis, which shall be considered sufficient and appropriate in accordance with the standard contained in the article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 Act of 20 July 2000 on the publication of normative acts and certain other acts. The subject matter of the proposed law is not covered by European Union law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u101	Thank you very much. Mr Krzysztof Paszyk, a club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u102	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I would like to present on behalf of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats Club a position on the Senate bill amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act, contained in print 3134. This project concerns the implementation of the request made in the citizens' petition P9/49/17 to the Senate to determine the end of the time limit laid down for the execution of the procedural action when the end of that period falls on a date recognised by the law as a day off from work. The calculation of terms in Polish law on the basis of various laws has always been a problem for both citizens and lawyers themselves. The rules are not detailed enough, the case law has proved divergent over time in this respect. The most problematic issue in this matter was the expiry of the deadline on Saturday, which since the 1980s has been really a day off from work in Poland. The fact that Saturday is in practice a day off from work does not, however, change the principles set out in the current Act of 1951, according to which Saturday is not a legal day off work. This has caused many problems in the calculation of time limits according to the rules applicable to particular areas of law. Some of these problems were solved by case law, although they were not uniform. The breakthrough came only in 2017, when the amendment of the Civil Code entered into force on 1 January and the amendment of the Code of Administrative Procedure on 1 June. Today is the time for the Code of Criminal Procedure, in which there is still an understatement in this regard. For example, in the order of the Supreme Court of the Criminal Chamber of 23 March 2016, we read that: Saturdays are not public holidays, but only the nature of the day in which a certain period is due to expire, causes ex lege to shift the performance of the activity to the next working day. The fact that the general courts are not in office on Saturdays in principle does not matter, as it is sufficient to send a letter before it expires in the post of the correspondence service provider in Poland or within the European Union. On the other hand, the Supreme Court took a different position in the order for a sign. II KZ 14/03, in which it stated that Saturdays are to be treated as a day recognised by the Act as a day off from work within the meaning of Article. 123 Code of Criminal Procedure. Similarly, in order II AKz 40/04, the Court of Appeal in Cracow stated that: Although the last day of the appeal was not a public day, the courts in the whole country did not work on that day - by appropriate order - the appellant could not lodge his complaint directly at the court's seat. It does not matter that some post offices were open that day, so the letter to the court could be sent by post. The bill presented today follows the line of the latter position, introducing in the Code of Criminal Procedure a solution similar to those previously introduced in the Civil Code and in the Code of Administrative Procedure. This will result in a codification of criminal and civil procedures, administrative procedures and tax procedures. The draft amendment envisages introducing a solution that will make Saturdays equal to work-free days. If the last day of the deadline falls on Saturday, it will be postponed to the day following the day or days off from work. The Polish People's Party Club - the Union of European Democrats is clearly in favour of further proceding this project and as a result accepting it. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u107	Thank you, Minister. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u108	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present the position of the club regarding the Senate draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure Act, print no.3134. The draft submitted regulates the determination of the end of the time limit laid down for the execution of the procedural action and leads to Saturday being considered as a day equivalent to the date recognised by the law as a day off from work. This project is the answer to the citizens' petition brought to the Senate by Mr Paul Janas. Currently, in criminal proceedings, the issue of the end of the deadline for Saturday is regulated differently than in civil, administrative and judicial proceedings. Consequently, the so-called. free Saturdays shall be the subject of a dispute. The Law amending the Labour Code adopted on 1 March 2001 introduced the statutory concept of a 5-day working week and resigned from the work-free Saturdays. This was also highlighted by the subsequent Supreme Court orders. For this reason, the Civil Code, the Code of Administrative Procedure, the Law of Procedure before administrative courts and the Tax Order included Saturday in a separate way and aligned it with days off from work. The lack of a uniform approach to the issue of free Saturdays is not justified. Therefore, the proposed proposal provides for a solution which will result in Saturdays in criminal proceedings being equaled to days off from work and consequently the last day on Saturday will be postponed to the day after the day or days off from work. There is no doubt that the substance of the proposal submitted will result in Members of Modern Members voting in favour of referring them to further work in committee.

The uncomplainable order of the court interferes directly with the interests of the victim by irreparably depriving him of the status of party in criminal proceedings. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u126	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The main objective of the proposed law, the shortest law commonly referred to as the Law on the Promotion of Rural Development of the RDPs 2014.2020, is to resolve the fundamental issue in relation to the possibility of introducing financial instruments - a new way of supporting farmers. Let me remind you that the Rural Development Programme is PLN 58 billion to be used by 2023. At the moment we already have 30% of the total amount and 16.5 billion in the accounts of farmers, entrepreneurs and local governments. The mechanisms at the moment are subsidies. There is both a grant system and a bonus system. A bonus system, i.e. one in which the total amount is non-refundable, and a subsidy system, i.e. one in which there is half of the amount, so you have to have an own contribution to make the investment, 50% the entire investment shall be repaid. The law on which we are proceeding is the introduction of a returnable financial instrument. And that's the first such fundamental goal. We are preparing to expand the scale of the beneficiaries. When implementing the Rural Development Programme, we have reached such a point that we need to look for opportunities for a group of beneficiaries not only for grant support. This mainly concerns farmers, but also entrepreneurs, small, medium and large, in rural areas, both those who can benefit from the development programme and - attention - those who cannot benefit from it because they have too much economic size, i.e. here the financial return instrument is also for large, unable to benefit from this form of support. The EU's analysis carried out on behalf of the Ministry of Agriculture has shown the appropriateness of introducing a guarantee financial instrument in the Rural Development Programme for the years 2014 and 2020 into the Rural Development Programme - a guarantee, as I have already said... It's a loan guarantee guaranteeing the possibility... of securing this loan. The beneficiary doesn't have to secure it, but it's secured here by this instrument. They are intended for farmers and businesses in the agri-food processing sector. The new form of support will complement the grant offer for the above mentioned groups. And the advantage of support in the form of a financial instrument, compared to grant forms, is the ability to generate from a specific allocation of resources a larger scale of investment, here in the agricultural and processing sector, through the so-called effect. leverage, i.e. the involvement of private banking sector funds. We are talking about the commitment of 50 million from the Rural Development Programme, which can be cyclically implemented over a number of years, and this will generate a credit mass that will be available at around 250 million, so this is quite serious so-called. the leverage. This proposal provides for amendments to the three laws: the first earlier mentioned by me, the basic law on support for rural areas, the second on corporate income tax and the third on guarantees and guarantees granted by the State Treasury and certain legal persons. As regards the first amendment relating to the Rural Development Support Act, it concerns the extension of the scope of the provisions covered by the Law on the rules for the implementation of financial instruments. Secondly, it adds the definitions of the new terms used in the proposed law: financial instruments and the final recipient and identifies the entities involved in the implementation of the financial instruments under the Rural Development Programme - the managing authority, the paying agency, the implementing bodies. It shall clarify the tasks of those entities, including the powers and control obligations, and shall indicate the additional elements to be covered by the agreement, the terms and conditions, the deadlines for the payment of funds, the conditions for the termination of the financing agreement, the conditions and the manner in which the funds are to be reimbursed and the obligation to be subject to controls by the competent institutions. In addition, the Act must specify the general procedure and deadline for the payment of funds by the paying agency to the beneficiaries. If necessary, I will be able to address the particular issues in which direction this is going in detail at the meeting of the Sejm Committee. That's it. quite precisely described in the explanatory memorandum of this law. In addition, the amendments to the Rural Development Support Act will consist in regulating the way in which the financial resources allocated to the financial contribution and interest and other income are handled and the profits generated by the marketing of those funds. Following the implementation of the financing obligations under the financing agreement as proposed in Article. 50d of the provision will be transferred to the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego for the provision of the National Guarantee Fund. Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego will be the parent bank, which will manage this - of course by signing agreements with cooperative banks and other most favourable banks - so that these funds can be implemented directly to them and that specific settlement agreements can be signed. As far as corporation tax is concerned, it is proposed in Article. 2 amendment of the Corporate Income Tax Act exempts from CIT the revenues of the entity implementing financial instruments under the RDP for the years 2014 to 2020. In accordance with Community rules on legal decisions relating to the entity's income, the revenues of the entity implementing the financial instrument derived from the resources of the RDP entrusted to that entity in the management of the financial instrument shall be allocated by the budget of the financial instrument. As always, financial terms are quite complicated, but it is a matter of ordering in the Income Tax Act the issue of the absence of income tax burden in relation to the financial instrument. It's the easiest way to reschedule. The same provision is currently in force with regard to the revenue of entities implementing financial instruments under cohesion policy. I will not hide that, when it comes to cohesion policy, we are looking for the right solutions and tools, and we are modeling on previous decisions, adapting them to the agricultural sector and agri-food processing, i.e. where needs are greatest. The third law, which is amended in connection with the introduction of financial instruments, is the Law on Guarantees and Guarantees provided by the State Treasury and certain legal persons. As proposed in the Article. 3 the amendment of the Law on Guarantees and Guarantees provided by the State Treasury and certain legal persons is intended to enable the Bank of National Farms to provide guarantees or guarantees to the agricultural and fisheries sectors under government programmes, including the implementation of the financial instruments of the RDP for the period 2014 to 2020. The proposed amendments also address a number of other issues. I will mention only a few of them: extending the existing definition of small and medium-sized enterprises entitled to benefit from guarantees or guarantees provided by the Bank of National Farms to include agricultural and fisheries activities, identifying the status of the Bank of National Farms guarantees or guarantees as a form of de minimis aid in agriculture or fisheries, giving up the obligation for the borrower to set up a micro-entrepreneur, small and medium-sized enterprise - safeguards for claims arising from the performance of the obligations of a guarantor or guarantor by Bank of National Farms. These are safeguards and decisions. The next is the introduction of a mandate for the Minister of Finance to issue regulations governing specific provisions and to provide a basis for the allocation of funds remaining after the implementation of financing agreements for the implementation of financial instruments as a possible additional source of supply to the National Guarantee Fund. I am convinced that we are building a good mechanism, which does not affect the existing financial structure, is rather piloting rather than a full, broad decision, which should be an important element in the new programming period as a tool to support a complementary financial offer in line with the needs of farmers and agri-food processing. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u128	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! To date, a grant scheme has been applied which has limited the funding possibilities of many operations and costs, including: the purchase of animals, annual plants or working capital. With investments to farmers, micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises in the agricultural and agri-food processing sectors, the launch of financial instruments in the RDPs for 2014 and 2020 will enable the request for a loan guaranteed by the scheme to the required own contribution necessary to receive the grant support. As a result, the beneficiary will be able to obtain a loan on preferential terms to finance the required own contribution. This will increase the availability of external sources of investment funding. The bill, as the Minister has already discussed quite extensively here, provides for amendments to three laws: the first - the law on supporting rural development, the second - the law on corporate income tax, and the third - the Law on guarantees and guarantees given by the State Treasury and certain legal persons. The essence of the amendment of the first of these laws is the introduction of provisions concerning the implementation of financial instruments, precisely guarantee instruments, and the identification of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego as the entity implementing financial instruments. The institutions cooperating with BGK will be cooperative banks and commercial banks. The amendment also includes provisions concerning the introduction of effective mechanisms or control systems, the determination of the procedure and the deadline for the payment of funds by the paying agency - this paying agency is, of course, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture - for the payment of these funds to beneficiaries, the conditions to be met by payment requests, etc. As the Minister discussed these two other laws very widely, the Corporate Income Tax Act and the amendments to the Guarantees and Guarantees Act, I already feel relieved of this obligation. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u132	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Rural Development Support Act with the participation of the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development under the Rural Development Programme for the years 2014 to 2020 and some other laws. The project provides for the introduction of a new form of support to be provided by financial instruments. The explanatory memorandum of the bill shows that the problem of difficult access for farmers to external sources of funding, which is a consequence of difficulties in making investments, slowing them down or liquidity disruptions, has been identified. The size of the financial gap was estimated at more than PLN 11 billion, and as a prescription for solving the external deficit, the Ministry proposes appropriate financial instruments to facilitate access to these external sources of funding. Unfortunately, as stated in the explanatory memorandum itself, the financial instruments implemented under the programme will not significantly reduce the financial gap. The planned EUR 50 million is a drop in the sea of needs. Small and medium-sized farms are colliding with the wall today because they are unable to overcome the problem of raising funds for development, for modernisation. It is becoming increasingly clear in the Polish countryside that there are large farms that have been able to invest, which are prospering and small and medium-sized farms are on the verge of profitability and either being liquidated or barely binding an end, most often thanks to households working outside the farm. There is still no idea how to help these farms, how to make the market a place for them to be profitable. I am afraid that the solution proposed in this project today, although it is heading in the right direction, will not produce the desired results due to the small scale. Farm debt is a huge problem today. According to the report of the National Debt Register of the Economic Information Office, the debt in the agricultural sector amounts to PLN 412.5 million and the average debt of the farmer amounts to approximately 49 thousand. zł. The farmer is a particular type of borrower due to the specific nature of his business, and this fact is most often not interested in banks. The adjustment of commitments by farmers depends on a number of factors, often independent of them, such as the abundance of crops depending on the weather situation, the price of agricultural fruits, the solvency of counterparties. The truth is, farmers pay when they get paid themselves. It is therefore more important for many farms to maintain relative financial stability, and any investment represents a major risk to this stability. On the other hand, the desire to modernise the farm is so strong that farmers forget the wise saying: money makes money and debt makes debt - and every investment should be aimed at improving the financial situation of the farm. At the same time, as far as the whole credit system is concerned, I note that a large part of the debt-related liability of farmers is borne by banks themselves, which are quite nonchalant in verifying the creditworthiness of farmers. The desire for profit often sends a realistic assessment of the situation, which, together with the other factors mentioned above, leads to a huge debt of farmers, with which Polish agriculture is currently struggling. In conclusion, I support the proposed solution, but I am very sceptical. on the effectiveness of the mechanism. Therefore, I ask the Minister to further, in-depth analysis of the problem and to take measures that will really contribute to supporting the development of Polish farms. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u152	In accordance with the draft law amending the Law on support for rural development involving the European Agricultural Fund for Rural Development under the Rural Development Programme for the period 2014 to 2020 and certain other laws submitted by the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development, farmers and micro-, small and medium-sized enterprises in the agri-food processing sector will be able to apply to banks involved in the implementation of financial instruments for a preferential loan covered by the guarantee from the financial instrument established in the framework of the RDPs 2014 to 2020. Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego is to secure loans to farmers who want to invest but do not have their own collateral through agreements with banks lending farmers. The introduction of regulations introduced by the bill is possible, among others. by carrying out additional studies by the European Investment Bank (EIB) on the feasibility of using financial instruments in the framework of the 2014-2020 RDPs. The proposed project was adopted by the Council of Ministers only a week ago. He was immediately referred to the first reading of the Sejm. However, it requires a broad analysis at least at the meeting of the Committee on Agriculture, as there has been no consultation with it. The procedure for the project is carried out instantly, as also indicated by the fact that the new regulations are to apply on the day following the date of their publication in the Official Journal. The Ministry of Agriculture's haste is due to the fear of less funding in the EU budget after 2020. According to government estimates, the funds planned to be made available for financial instruments of EUR 50 million are to create a range of new loans of at least EUR 250 million by the end of 2023. In order to make full use of these opportunities, potential beneficiaries will need to be encouraged to do so. The problem with the loans that farmers are taking is the instability of the agricultural sector dependent on a number of factors (drying, ASF) and emerging debts as a result of their occurrence. The banks involved are to obtain in this case a credit risk component of up to 80% for one credit. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u158	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Law and Justice Club a draft law amending the excise duty refund Act contained in the price of diesel used for agricultural production, print no. 3206. It is a very important act primarily for Polish farmers, food producers. It concerns the possibility of obtaining a refund of part of the excise duty included in the price of gas oil purchased and used for agricultural production from all types of gas oil offered on the market. This is to add to the catalogue of diesel classified under CN code 27102011. On 9 November 2018, the Sejm adopted a law amending certain laws to introduce simplifications for entrepreneurs in tax and economic law. The exclusion from the diesel catalogue of CN code 27102011 makes it impossible from 1 January 2019 to refund excise duty in the case of the purchase of this oil for agricultural production. It is therefore necessary to introduce a change that will allow farmers to return part of their excise duties. The envisaged solution is beneficial for agricultural producers primarily because of the possibility of obtaining a refund of part of the excise duty included in the price of gas oil purchased and used for agricultural production. This amendment should enter into force on the day following the publication of the Act, with effect from 1 January 2019. This proposal does not entail a burden from the state budget or from the budget of local government units. The project has a positive social, financial and legal impact. The subject matter of the proposed regulation is not covered by European Union law. High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I ask the High House to pass this law. The club supports this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 3 The Real Estate Management Act to the extent that it makes the request made by the former co-owner of the expropriated property or its heirs subject to the consent of the other former co-owners of the property or their heirs. The second sentence is that of 12 December 2017, in which the non-constitutionality of the Article was declared. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. In the first sentence, to the extent that it excludes the right of the previous owner or heir to claim the reimbursement of the property acquired by the Treasury or the local government unit by means of an agreement referred to in Article. 114, when the property became redundant for a public purpose justifying its acquisition. The project provides for the possibility of a return to the property which has become redundant for public purposes. In the event of non-refund proceedings by the other entitled parties, their claim for reimbursement shall expire. In addition, the project provides for a limitation in time for the recovery of expropriated property, which will close the period of uncertainty as to the legal situation of the parties concerned and allow the use of the property for purposes other than those specified in the expropriation decision. After the changes, each previous owner or his/her heirs will be able to obtain a refund of the expropriated property regardless of whether the other previous joint owners or their heirs have applied for repayment. The notification of the other entitled pursuant to the new provision shall be made by means of a notice. If, within the prescribed period, the other entitled persons do not submit an application for reimbursement of their participation, their entitlement shall expire. Any previous owner or heir will be able to claim the refund of the expropriated property regardless of the form of acquisition of the property right. The right to return the property will be valid after 20 years. The right to reimbursement shall expire if an appropriate application is not submitted within that period. This means limiting the possibility of recovery of expropriated properties during the period. The termination of the right should close the period of uncertainty as to the legal situation of the parties concerned and create the possibility of using expropriated property but not for public purposes as set out in the expropriation decision for other purposes, e.g. housing. The proposed solution will reduce the number of requests for the return of expropriated immovable property acquired or acquired in the past than 20 years ago. This solution meets the expectations of local government units and associations of tenants. At the same time, it was envisaged that the law could be applied within 3 years from the date of entry into force of the Act with a request for repayment of the expropriated property if, on the date of entry into force of the Act, 20 years had elapsed since the date on which the decision to expropriate it became final, and if no more than 3 years had elapsed by that date. The project is absolutely right - it is an assessment of many specialists in real estate law. Finally, the expected limitation of the claim for reimbursement after 20 years will be introduced, as it is difficult to understand the meaning of the regulation allowing reimbursement after 60 years of expropriation. The draft law does not require notification of the aid scheme. The regulations introduced do not constitute public aid: the proposed rules do not relate to the flow of funds, they do not provide an economic advantage for the public authorities. The project complies with European Union legislation and does not require presentation to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union. It's one of those billing projects, maybe a few, uncontroversial ones, I think. It is expected by different environments, by citizens. Therefore, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u187	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mrs Małgorzata Chmiel, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u188	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Club, I present an opinion on the government proposal for a law on amending the Real Estate Management Act. The aim of the project is to implement the judgments of the Constitutional Tribunal of 14 July 2015 and 12 December 2017. In its judgment of 14 July 2015, the Constitutional Tribunal pointed out the non-constitutionality of the provisions requiring the consent of all joint owners of the property concerned or their heirs when applying for repayment of the expropriated property. The amendment of the Act introduces the possibility to seek the return of the expropriated property by each co-owner individually, irrespective of the consent of the other owners. The Constitutional Tribunal also held that it was not possible to repay the expropriated property by contract. Until now, it has been possible to return the expropriated property through an administrative decision. The draft amendment therefore provides for the possibility of returning the expropriated property through both the administrative decision and the contract. In addition, the purpose of the draft law is to guarantee legal certainty in respect of immovable property covered by the Article. 136 of the Real Estate Management Act - this is an article about the possibility of returning the expropriated property over a period of 7 years in the absence of construction or 10 years in the absence of completion - by introducing a 20-year period after which the entitlement to return the expropriated property expires. After these 20 years, you will no longer be able to return the property. The aim of the proposed solution is to balance the private interest and the public interest. On the one hand, the proposed solution restricts the possibility of seeking the return of expropriated properties at the time, while on the other hand it provides a three-year transitional period in which entitled persons may claim the return of expropriated properties. However, we have doubts as to whether the limitation to 20 years of time in which the possibility of returning expropriated property will not be detrimental to expropriated owners or their heirs. Now this time is not defined, but there are different family and inheritance circumstances. A 3-year transition period may also be insufficient. However, these are positive changes resulting from the decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal, which is why the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club will vote in favour of this law. Thank you.

Among these negative developments, we particularly want to highlight, firstly, the intention to deprive people with disabilities of the possibility of voting in correspondence, of which, after social protests, some have been withdrawn, while retaining a limited number of correspondence voting, only for persons with a judgment of a significant or moderate degree of disability, and this means that, above all, the elderly and others, without a ruling, are currently unable to vote in correspondence. To this end, Mr Łukasz Schreiber, on 14 December 2017 in the Sejm on behalf of the Members of the PIS, pointed out that disabled people can trade votes. Secondly, the intention to deprive disabled people of the possibility of voting by correspondence was not an accident or accident at work. PiS had previously demanded that people with disabilities vote in correspondence as opposed to the constitution, as well as that of a proxy vote. Thirdly, the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 21 October 2014 on the benefit to guardians of adult disabled persons was still not implemented, and, after all, the implementation of that judgment in 2013, which was almost 6 years ago, was requested by PiS Members represented before the Constitutional Tribunal by Marek Asta and Stanisław Swed. The government of PO-PSL at the end of its term of office in 2015 prepared a draft law implementing that judgment and reserved the funds needed for the implementation of the law in the budget for 2016. However, despite more than 3 years of government of the PiS, the sentence has not yet been passed. completed and will not be completed until the end of the term of office in the absence of time. Fourthly, the PiS argues that it cannot comply with the abovementioned judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal or take various other measures for the benefit of persons with disabilities until there is a new system of case-law on the degree of disability. However, according to the order of the President of the Council of Ministers of February 2017, the draft of the new system of case law was to be ready by 31 March 2018 at the latest, i.e. a year ago. When this deadline passed, further scheduled dates were given: first June 2018, then September 2018. All deadlines have passed, and the project remains classified. All we know is that the president of the ZUS was appointed and was handed over to Minister Elizabeth Rafalska. It is therefore clear that there will be no new disability case law in this term of office. Fifthly, Prime Minister Beata Szydło and Minister Elżbieta Rafalska announced at the press conference in January 2017 that 5,000 would be set up in the course of the year. new jobs for people with disabilities in public institutions. After that, however, it was never returned to the case, and to this day it is not known where these places are. Finally, for the first time in 10 years in 2017, the employment of people with disabilities in the civil service decreased by 233 people, although at the same time total employment in the civil service increased by 534 posts and, in terms of directorial and managerial positions, this employment increase was 52%. Therefore, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition will vote against information because the Charter of the Rights of Persons with Disabilities is grossly violated and the rights of persons with disabilities in our country are seriously threatened. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u233	Thank you very much, sir. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u234	Caring for people with disabilities or independence is one of the greatest challenges facing modern social policy. At the same time, it is one of the most recent and least explored sectors of broadly understood social security. This is due both to the fact that care for independent persons is traditionally concentrated within the household and to the fact that the social care area is specifically intersected with the healthcare area. Compared to other European countries, Polish policies on care for independent people are still in the development phase. It is very important to remove barriers. A recent study by the State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities shows that the population of people with disabilities is not uniform. There are various problems that the subjects face, but also ways to deal with them. Looking at the lives of people with disabilities through the prism of indicators synthesizing information on quality of life in different areas, it should be noted that the situation is not good. The global quality of life index, which is an average value of sub-indices, has reached a value of 38,5 points per 100 possible. Among the spontaneously identified areas of need, three were dominated, indicated by more than 15% surveyed: first, needs in the area of housing and self-living - 17% , in particular to assist an assistant or nurse in daily activities, adjusting the apartment to the individual needs of the disabled person concerned. The needs in this area were most important for people with mobility disabilities - 25% and for people with intellectual disabilities - 23%. While it was particularly important for people with disabilities to adapt their housing to the individual needs of a disabled person and to have a lower floor or ground floor accommodation, for persons with intellectual disabilities the key in this area was helping an assistant to perform daily activities, relieve the burden of carers, as well as temporary replacement to enable the carer to take up work at least to a small extent or just rest. The second most frequently identified needs were related to instruments, devices or technologies -16%, among others. It is about orthopaedic or optical equipment, equipment that allows, improves hearing and computer equipment, but also various types of everyday devices that help to eliminate disability-related limitations. This area was most important for people with visual impairment - 24%, hearing dysfunction - 20% and with motor disability - 15% . In all these cases, higher interest rates were recorded among people with a significant degree of disability. Another important issue identified by the respondents is the need for transport and movement - 16% , mainly the elimination of architectural barriers in public space, but also the adaptation of the means of transport are especially important for people with motion dysfunction - 25%, as well as with visual dysfunction -14%. The remaining needs of all respondents did not reach more than 9% indications. However, it should be noted that their validity is much higher among groups of disabled people with certain types of disability. In our view, government institutions dealing with the assistance of people with disabilities should focus on these points. Thank you very much. Blue cards. It should be made clear that almost 18,000 are involved. of them, it wasn't the right thing to do. It has been recognised that the law requires thorough review so that procedures do not stigmatise families that need to be protected in crisis situations and which need to be helped effectively. Violence in the family is most often affected by weak people and those who are harder to defend: women, children, older people, also people with disabilities. In 2017, the Council The police reported 165,000. cases of domestic violence in which nearly 93 000 have been injured. persons. 73% Victims are women, more than 14% - children. On 12 December 2017, Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki stressed the need for a policy of firm opposition to any manifestations of domestic violence and the urgent need for profound changes to support those who experience domestic violence. However, the amendment, which was proposed at the end of 2018, actually took us back years to combat and prevent family violence. There is one municipality, Zakopane, beloved by Prime Minister Beata Szydło, who praised the council for not adopting such a program. The minister, who is to deal with those most in need of help, praised the municipality, which did not introduce a resolution against domestic violence. And this is, ladies and gentlemen, a scandal. Violence has sex. Its victims are mostly women. This is due to the stereotypes that have been established over the years, the unequal distribution of power between men and women. And the fight against violence in the family must be a cross-party and long-term issue. Violence affects regardless of the place of residence, education, views or sympathy or political antipathy. We will not build a true democracy and an open state until half of society is threatened by violence until violence is effectively prosecuted and punished until this subject is taken seriously until we change the stereotypical thinking about the role of men and women. It is necessary to correctly define the phenomenon of violence in the family. According to Art. 3 lit. b of the Istanbul Convention, domestic violence means any physical, sexual, psychological or economic violence occurring in the family or household, or between former or present spouses or partners, whether the perpetrator or victim shares the place of residence or not. The anti-violence law in the family needs to be revised. It was to be revised in 2015, but it didn't happen, and it probably won't happen this year either. She needs to be updated after 10 years of action. It must be updated in cooperation with all actors who work with victims, perpetrators, lawmakers and practitioners, but also with non-governmental organisations that have been effectively helping victims of domestic violence for years with low budgets. The second thing, very important, is a credible study of the phenomenon of violence. We do not know the real data about violence that occurs in Poland, we do not know its scale. We don't even know how many women a year die of domestic violence every month. No one's running statistics like this. We can expect these women to be around 400 and 500 a year, but there might as well be a lot more women. Third point: real cooperation and funding of non-governmental organisations helping victims of violence. It cannot be that the Women's Rights Centre, the Blue Line or other organizations deal with financial problems in a month. It's these organizations that are closest to people, they help them. Point four: implementation of the anti-violence convention to change the definition of rape, to introduce provisions on economic violence, to introduce provisions to better isolate the perpetrator of violence from the victim. The draft law on economic violence lies in the Sejm for almost a year, was not even read at the committee's deliberations or at the Sejm meeting. The other two projects are prepared. It cannot be that a person who experiences violence has to flee from his own home sometimes to a place many miles away. We need to take the victim's side, not the victim's side. Fifth point: mandatory for perpetrators of domestic violence to participate in correction and education programmes. According to the Ministry's research, as many as 67% of the perpetrators who participated in such therapies declared that during their duration they had not used violence for more than one year, 34% participants in these programmes have completely ceased their application. These are beautiful numbers, these are people who have struggled with their problems, but this therapy cannot be optional. Sixth point: training for workers of the actors identified in the Act on the Prevention of Domestic Violence, Social Welfare, Police, Teachers, Continuing Training, Assistance and Support, so that they understand the problem of domestic violence, help not only victims but also perpetrators. Point seven: regular social campaigns that involve the prevention of violence, but which also say that men and women are equal, that we need to move away from stereotypical thinking about the division of roles or the division of power between men and women, that violence just comes from the fact that we think that someone is worse, that someone is different. Point eight: a change in the functioning of the Justice Fund, so that money from it can actually come to the aid of victims and support for those leaving prison. 25 million PLN from the fund went to CBA, 100 million PLN went to voluntary firefighters, 800,000 PLN. Tadeusz Rydzyk's foundation. The fund is intended to serve victims of violence, this is money that we can really use to help them. Point nine: decent sexual and anti-discrimination education in schools. This is something that from childhood will teach that a man and woman are equal, that no violence is allowed at home, but will also teach where to look for help. It is a large, comprehensive document, recommendations developed with NGOs. This document is waiting for you to read it to try to implement it. This is what NGOs are waiting for, and so are we. We want to support this legislative process, work on solutions for, among others. anti-violence. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u272	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary bill we are debating. This proposal provides clarification of the legislation adopted by the High House on 28 December, which is commonly known as a mechanism blocking the rise in energy prices, which was then prepared by the Ministry of Energy and the Government. This common name is as suitable as possible, because the aim of working on this law at the end of last year was to block the increase in electricity and protect Polish families, companies, industry and local governments from the negative effects of the surge in electricity prices that we see across Europe. We have achieved this goal, which I would like to emphasise very strongly here. Energy prices, electricity prices in Poland will not increase as in many other European countries, where we record increases not only by a few dozen percent, but even more than 100%. Let me remind you that this record increase in electricity prices, which we must protect ourselves from, is linked to the European Union's climate policy. This was implemented by the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 13 October 2003. This Directive established trade in greenhouse gas emission allowances within the Community and imposed an obligation to purchase emission allowances by sources of combustion of fuels. As a result, the price of allowances, which in 2017, oscillated around EUR 5.6, jumped to EUR 25 last September. This was an increase of more than 400%, which inevitably influenced electricity prices in most EU countries. We in Poland have maintained energy prices at the current level acceptable to consumers. The protective umbrella, which is adopted 2 months ago, is wide enough to stop prices wherever there is pressure for a raise, from households to businesses and local governments. The legislative process that we are continuing today concerns clarification and elimination of interpretations distorting the intentions of the legislator. The aim is to clarify the rules and dispel the concerns of the actors involved in the activities covered by the Act, inter alia. President of the Energy Regulatory Office. The Act also gives the possibility to apply for the payment of the energy price difference to the final consumer who does not use trading companies but buys on his own on the stock exchange. The amendment also refers to the deadline for amending contracts with the date of adoption of the regulation by the Minister of Energy, creating good conditions for companies to effectively adapt the turnover of contracts linking them with recipients to the provisions of the Act. It is worth mentioning that the December project did not assume interference with the power regulator's powers from the beginning. The legislator only imposed a new, 95% reduction in the level of the transitional fee, leaving the rest of the network tariff setting process under the existing rules. The revision, by deleting the regulation on network tariffs in relation to the 2018 rate level, simplifies the system while ensuring that the transitional fee is reduced without increasing the burden for final customers on transmission and distribution charges compared to last year. The aim of the Act remains unchanged: to protect Poles from rising electricity prices. The amendment also provides that all types of electricity supply contracts will be covered by the fixed price. This was also the intention of passing a law at the beginning. The amendment aims to clarify the rules in order to eliminate interpretations that delete certain types of contracts for the sale of energy, e.g. such as would be considered convenient in terms of their interests for the omission of the trading company. Ladies and gentlemen! Today we discussed this proposal in detail at the meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury. The Commission adopted a draft law without a vote against. On the other hand, we also draw conclusions from those voices that appeared, talks about improving the bill to make it clearer. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I make seven amendments to this law and ask the High House to accept them. Earlier, of course, the Committee on Energy and the Treasury will debate this. Thank you so much for listening. Your Marshal is handing over the amendments to the bill. [[Applause]]
