We proceed with the examination of item 32 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and some other laws (Print No 3137). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Łukasz Piebiak to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u302	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The primary objective of the draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code and certain other laws contained in print 3137 submitted by the Council of Ministers to the High House is to improve and speed up the judicial investigation in civil matters. The information coming to the Ministry of Justice gives rise to the assessment that the efficiency and speed of judicial proceedings in civil matters in relation to the measures involved is not satisfactory and, above all, does not meet social expectations. In general, this is due to the lack of compliance of court procedures largely from the 19th century with the requirements of the post-industrial information society. The current model of civil proceedings is not flexible enough to allow for a swift settlement of the dispute while respecting the principles of fair trial and any procedural guarantees. Today's civil proceedings are not immune to disorganizing factors, especially to procedural obstruction. The proposed amendment to the Civil Procedure Code takes up the challenge of breaking the difficulties that the previous governments have not noticed. The proposed solutions are largely based on proposals presented during public debates, such as: a change in the institution so called reservations to the protocol. The purpose of the various changes was assessed not only from a doctrinal point of view, but also from the point of view of practical effects on civil justice through the use of practitioners' opinions. The first area in which the proposal introduces significant changes is the organisation of the court's work on the case. In place of an organisation based on a mandatory hearing model, but without a separate preparatory phase, a flexible model is introduced to adapt the method of investigation to the circumstances of a particular case. The possibility of adversarial ruling outside the hearing will be used more widely. In addition, the new model of organisation of proceedings puts a stronger emphasis on achieving a settlement of the dispute than before. The model provides for the drawing up of a hearing plan - in those cases which require it because of the complexity or complexity - which sets out the parties' demands and objections in detail - to determine the issues at issue, it already sets out the evidence necessary to carry out the case and, above all, sets the time limits for the meetings and the probable time limit for the decision. Changes in the organisation of the hearing also include the principle that the judgment should take place after the first date of the hearing and, if that is not possible, subsequent time limits for the hearing should be fixed on the following days either directly or closely thereafter, in order to avoid multiple-month breaks between meetings. Another area in which the project changes the current process reality is the fight against procedural obstruction. It is regrettable to say that procedural obstruction has become an exception if not a rule, it is certainly a common phenomenon. It's not. What is surprising, however, is that in the current legal situation there is no regulation allowing such behaviour to be combated or prevented. Obstructive behaviours are collectively defined as abuse of procedural law and defined as the deliberate exercise of their procedural rights in a manner incompatible with the purpose for which they serve. On the basis of experience from practice, abuses have been identified, the effects of which are most detrimental to the efficiency of the proceedings: a chain of complaints on the same issue, chains of requests to exclude a judge based on the same circumstances or requests for interpretation of the judgment. Instruments have been provided for such proceedings by the court to prevent the intended delay in the recognition of the case. Unsubstantiated letters aiming only at delay in proceedings will, of course, be able to leave the court without recognition in the file of the case. On the other hand, the charge of set-off, which has so far been a very effective tool of procedural obstruction, is subject to restrictions aimed at eliminating the charges of set-off in bad faith. The proposal also significantly changes the regulation of separate procedures in the civil process, i.e. procedures for identifying cases with specific characteristics. First of all, there is a re-introduction of a separate procedure in economic matters, which, on the one hand, by greater rigour, motivates participants to carry out evidence and, on the other hand, opens up opportunities to use information technology more efficiently than ever before. In a simplified procedure, the scope of which is significantly extended, mechanisms are put in place to allow less stringent treatment of the obligations of evidence in order to speed up the procedure. In electronic reprimanding proceedings, the link between this procedure and the ordinary process, which has so far caused numerous problems in contact with traditional judiciary, is relaxed. The regulation of prescriptive and reprimanding proceedings shall be structured. In addition to these three major changes, the project includes a number of improvements concerning the various aspects of civil proceedings that will allow the existing reserves in the justice system to be used. Firstly, it is a change in the jurisdiction of the court, which is pro-consumer on the one hand, as it requires that cases against the consumer be brought before the court in which the consumer's district is resident. On the other hand, this reduces the impact on the most heavily charged large-town courts, which should help to offset the burden on courts to work across the country. The project introduces a number of new solutions to the investigation of evidence, including in particular the possibility of negotiating the remuneration of experts, allows witnesses to make written statements, makes greater use of the evidence contained in court files or by other public authorities. As a complement to the existing possibility of issuing a judgment without a hearing since 2016, the draft provides as a rule for issuing rules at classified meetings. In many cases, the current obligation to hold meetings is replaced by the obligation to hear the parties, which is more flexible because they can be carried out outside the meeting, including in writing. The project limits formalism when announcing and justifying judgments, which will allow judges to focus on their remaining responsibilities. This concerns, in particular, the withdrawal from the obligation to publish judgments and provisions at a sitting which no one has put themselves before, i.e. an empty courtroom, and the requirement to be concise with the court's justifications. It is also a principle that provisions issued at a secret meeting shall be justified only at the request of the party. This will save the work of judges and courts and thus speed up proceedings. The proposal provides for simplification of the procedure for the recognition of remedies. On the one hand, these amendments are based on the fact that some of the complaints will be dealt with by another composition of the same court which issued the contested decision, without the need to transfer the whole of the file together with the appeal to the Court of Appeal, i.e. the so-called. horizontal complaints. On the other hand, the principle was adopted that the review of the appeal brought, such as the examination of the timeliness, the shortcomings of formal and fiscal remedies addressed to the Court of First Instance, is carried out only by the court, that is, the court of second instance, and not, so far, also by the Court of First Instance. In the context of the Court of First Instance, the powers of formal review of remedies have been extended to referenda. It is designed to extend the powers of referents who will also be authorised to act in appeals courts, to issue orders for payment in court proceedings and to act in most of the registers kept by courts. Changes in court fees are primarily aimed at simplifying the system of fees, strengthening their incentive role. Fees should be designed so that cost-effective procedural actions lead to the most efficient solution to the dispute. The project also provides for a clear preference for consumers, workers and households. Consumer rights are expected to be so called. a fee bonus, i.e. a reduction in the claim fee resulting from an attempt to settle a dispute before an action is brought. The project also contains a number of minor changes concerning individual civil process institutions, whose common aim is to save the time and work required of the court and, above all, of the parties for the effective enforcement of their rights. In summary, the proposed changes are intended to make civil proceedings more effective, efficient and cheaper and thus more accessible to the average citizen. These changes are not only necessary but necessary for the smooth functioning of the justice system. I therefore request that the High House adopt the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u306	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - the Civic Coalition - I wanted to put forward a position on the draft contained in print 3137. The draft law on the amendment of the Civil Procedure Code Act was divided into 10 parts within which the reasons for the proposed amendments were presented. Individual bodies of reasons shall include: preparation of the hearing, prevention of abuse of procedural law, matters of evidence, justification of judgments and appeal proceedings, proceedings in economic matters, other separate proceedings, general improvement of proceedings, proceedings to safeguard judicial costs, introductory and transitional provisions. The justification for the project is 210 pages, and the text of the amendment is placed on 69 pages. As a result, we are dealing, at least for the sake of the figures given, with a comprehensive revision of civil procedural law, as well as some other laws which are amended here: the ownership of premises, the general court system, the claim in group proceedings, the bailiffs. The existing Civil Procedure Code was amended several times after 1989. These were both amendments with a deep, even model structure, nature, as well as changes that were less important. However, they have all been done in an evolutionary manner over recent years. The overall objective of the proposed project is, as a result of the content of the statement of reasons, to improve and, literally, to accelerate civil proceedings and to improve the situation of the citizen present in the court. The justification for the project states that according to the data of the Minister of Justice, the number of cases affecting civil courts is increasing, as a result of which time is extended for cases to be dealt with in the first instance. The data presented in the explanatory memorandum would, of course, require a deeper analysis of the reasons for the case: whether delays in the examination of cases are not a consequence of the ministry's policy in holding the appointments of judges, resulting in a decreasing number of judges. The justification for the project also points to other shortcomings in the justice system. The views are expressed that the proposed amendment to the Code of Civil Procedure constitutes the most fundamental amendment of civil procedural law since the adoption of the Code of Civil Procedure. The view would only be so true if we measured the scale of these changes by the number of pages on which the changes and even the number of changes were placed. In reality, however, it is different. A large proportion of the changes contained in the project are of secondary importance or technical nature. The proposed amendments include a change of technical nature without a substantive consequence of changes to the rules, e.g. the change of concepts from the facts to facts or from the case to an accident or in the event of an accident. You can also ask questions about the validity of the amendment, e.g. Article 2 355 c.p.c. concerning the termination of proceedings. Undoubtedly, some of the changes have nothing to do with speeding up civil proceedings or improving the position of a citizen in court. On the contrary, it seems that some solutions have even worsened the existing status of the parties in the proceedings. To sum up this part of the statement: it seems that this random range of changes gives rise to the belief that the design shape is a generalization of the practical experience of the designers - I do not claim that it does not matter. Numerous and frequent, sometimes accidental changes to code rules are not desirable. Unfortunately, within the framework of the no longer existing Committee on Civil Law Codification, acting at the Minister of Justice, acting first under the direction of Prof. Zygmunt Radwański, and then Prof. Tadeusz Ereciński, the idea arose that the current amendments to civil law should be limited to the minimum necessary, but work on the new modern Code of Civil Procedure. These works, let us add, have been undertaken, but have been abandoned with the actual liquidation of the codification committee. The amendment of the Code, which would pursue such important objectives as set out in the explanatory memorandum, should not be of an incidental and clipping nature, but should fall within the scope of the comprehensive and complete procedural regulation of the new Code of Civil Procedure. Any change in the model of civil proceedings should be preceded by a profoundly substantive discussion involving representatives of all legal professions and scientific backgrounds, and the project itself should be thoroughly refined. Hurrying here is a harmful thing. An example of a solution that seems to be subject to deeper regulation is the proposal to restore it to c.p.c. provisions on separate economic proceedings. The only argument in favour of restoring this procedure is the extension of the economic proceedings after 3 May 2012. The provisions in force before that date have been examined in a manner known to the Constitutional Court and the solutions currently proposed should also be assessed in this respect. In addition, the project submitted does not comply with the principle, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that subjecting the case to a decision should guarantee its maximum objectivity. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u310	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The revision of the Civil Procedure Code is necessary, but it should be borne in mind that excessive concentration of procedural material may be associated with formalism rather than with a thorough examination of the actual circumstances and the issuing of a fair ruling. ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u314	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! In answer to the Members' questions, I will say that the answer to the first question is very simple and, I think, responds to the expectations of both the Member and a much larger group of people, i.e. Polish citizens or all parties, who appear before the Polish courts, because they are not only Polish citizens. It is precisely in this proposal that we introduce the principle that the first delivery of the pleading from the court, whether it be a payment order or simply a copy of the application, will be a service which will have to be certain in some way, i.e. the consignment will have to be picked up by the addressee or an adult householder, as it is today, but we exclude the so-called. delivery to aviso, something that caused many human tragedies, many intervention materials in the media, because sometimes dishonest plaintiffs gave outdated or even fictitious addresses. The mailman didn't check 'cause he didn't have it or he didn't want it. It's been a lot of things. In any case, in the end, it happened as the Member said that the citizen had learned from the bailiff that there was a trial against him and that he had lost it and had to pay. And it was often a number of times higher amounts than the ones he might have owed. We introduce the principle that this first service is to be an effective service, that is to say, to the hands of the addressee or the householder, not on an aviso, but possibly through a bailiff. We come from this assumption that since the bailiff can find a debtor to carry out execution operations, he will also be able to find the defendant in a civil trial and deliver him the first pleading in the case. It will protect just ordinary people whose interests should simply be protected. Of course, there could be a charge. - even somewhere, in some statement, not by the Member, but by another of the members present, the representatives of the clubs, in the speech that fell here, one could hear something like this in part - that it would not speed up the procedure or maybe even slow it down. However, when these values are compared, i.e. the protection of rights, which ensures real service, and the speed of conduct, it becomes clear that the first is a higher value. Even if this service by the bailiff would slow down a little bit, it would be worthwhile to bear this cost so that we really have to deal with a civilized, law-abiding process. This is the kind of trial we all expect. The second question was how to treat writings that are not written by professionals and therefore are not always understandable to the court. That, of course, is a powerful problem. The principle in the Code of Civil Procedure, which we do not change, because it would be difficult to imagine, in some way it could be changed, is that we take into account the author's writing and intention, not how this letter was titled and whether or not any particular formalisms were preserved when drawing up this letter. So clearly is the Code of Civil Procedure, and that has been the case for years. Of course, it should not be so that because of the non-professional nature of the site there are some negative consequences. The courts should rule in accordance with this principle, but this is a matter of practice. I hope that rare are cases where the courts, I do not know, are guided by some excessive formalism in assessing the content of the letter. Of course, a panacea to these problems would be the introduction of solicitor-radical coercion, but it could again hit those who could not afford a lawyer or counsel, and would not have financial resources or even knowledge or belief that such assistance should simply be sought. This is, of course, a difficult problem. I hope that another law, which has already fortunately passed through the High House and has begun to function, i.e. a law amending the Law on Free Legal Assistance, in which a full catalogue of persons entitled to free legal assistance has been introduced, will make such situations as you mention rarer, because just anyone who makes a statement - and it is in force since 1 January - that he cannot afford to pay legal aid from his own resources, is entitled to public-funded legal aid. We are talking about professionals, lawyers, lawyers who should formulate correspondence addressed to the court in such a way that the party cannot be affected by the negative consequences of its legal ignorance. After all, not all citizens are lawyers. Most of them aren't, and they don't have to be. Thank you very much.

These tests are really needed for patients, they are needed for patients. This percentage is less than 1% it places Poland in the tail of all Western European countries, where on average 30% of these researches are on average. There are costs in a non-commercial clinical trial, and there is no sponsor. It is a study that helps patients in difficult situations, in the case of very many different diseases, such as malignancies, chronic diseases, rare diseases. This is often the last resort for patients for whom conventional therapy is already over. There is a pressing need to increase the number of these studies. I would like to stress that these studies, according to the law in force in Poland, can run medical universities, hospitals. In everyday practice, however, these are multicentre, international projects. It's in child cancer, which I'm honored to represent. In fact, no Polish child can be treated properly if it is not carried out in such a non-sponsored clinical trial. How is this happening right now? This is the way it is done: the president of the university or the director, who currently has no money, has to be sponsored. There is no profit in such research. This is not a research conducted by a pharmaceutical company. The only profit is what the sick get. Therefore, there is a very urgent need to increase the number of these studies, to facilitate the opening of studies, to secure funds for programme insurance, registration costs, data manager costs, monitor costs. Although this is not a big cost, because already a lion's part of this research is covered by the National Health Fund, Poland falls out of the calculation of common conclusions, and if it does not enter such a project, it loses the possibility of consulting its patients in various fields. I hope that the Agency for Medical Research, which is being created, will solve this problem and allow Poland not to remain in the tail of Europe. Father Zdzisław Pałubicki, because he was born on 11 May 1935 in Liniewa, Kashubach. Soon he and his family lived in Nakle. He attended a local middle school. After his graduation, he joined a seminary in Gniezno, where on May 23, 1959, at the hands of cardinals. Stefan Wyszyński was ordained priestly. He was a diocesan priest for a year. He served as a vicar in the parish in Szubina. At that time he thought about going to Africa and then joined the Jesuit Order. Her missionary vocation was due to her mother, who prayed that her son would become a missionary. How often he said: When I was three years old, my mother died. After my priestly ordination, I learned from my aunts that my mother had prayed that I would become a missionary. Father Zdzisław Pałubicki has carried out over 1 thousand since 1963. Weekly missions, which means 25,000. sciences in Poland and various parts of the world, including: in Australia, Canada, Sweden, Portugal, Italy, Palestine, Belarus, Austria, Great Britain or America. At that time the courage cost, but it was not exposed by him in order to gain cheap applause. His views and deeply patriotic sermons and uncompromising towards communism caused the church authorities to forbid him from preaching God's word in fear of his life, for he could share the tragic fate of priests of patriots tormented by the communist regime. Even during the period of the Polish People's Republic there appeared information that Father Zdzisław Pałubicki is on a special list of priests, i.e. on the target of the Security Service. He's been interrogated several dozen times by SB officers. His passport was refused in order to prevent him from going on missions where he was to preach the teachings of God's Mercy. He has developed a four-volume manual for the mission of Divine Mercy. In 1988, in Rome, he personally handed it to his father St. John Paul II. He also published the missionary prayer book, the indulgences--the manifestation of Divine Mercy, or the prayer in the words of St. Faustina and other publications. Michał Sopoćki - Confessor of Sister Faustina. It was this image that received the first approval of the Polish Episcopate commission on 5 October 1954 on the 16th anniversary of the death of St. Faustina's sisters. Father Zdzisław Pałubicki since 1992 belonged to the Jesuit community in Kalisz, where he worked at the Shrine of the Heart of Jesus the Merciful. He often went away with folk missions and engaged in the spreading of the cult of St. Andrzej Bobola and the cult of Divine Mercy. His missionary work contributed greatly to the development of the service to the Most Holy Heart of Jesus, popularizing the silhouette of St. Andrzej Boboli also developed the cult of Divine Mercy in many parishes. Father Zdzisław Pałubicki died on 15 February this year. in Kalisz in the memory of Blessed. Fr. Michał Sopoćki in 84 years of religious life and 60 years of priesthood. The objective of this article, taking into account recently the tensions of some politicians in relations between the two states, is closer to an indeterminate one, but this seems to be a hidden political objective. This causes great damage to residents who are proud of a tolerant, open city respecting the achievements of many generations and many cultures that influenced the strong and beautiful historical position of Świdnica. At this point I would like to thank the Ministry of Foreign Affairs very warmly for their very rapid response and protest against this phenomenon. I hope it's an incident that won't happen again, and the people will still be proud of their multicultural, multi-faith history. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-02-20-sejm-77-3.u392	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I was obliged by a group of voters to make this statement. I very much regret that it is now the middle of the night, midnight, and there is no Minister Ziobra, Minister Wójcik and Minister Jaki. I hope that this speech somehow gets to them after all. Minister Ziobra's department is preparing a project quite thorough reform of the family and care code. This project is advertised, recommended, praised as a project that will restore, and will actually introduce the possibility of alternate care in Poland. This is a very good direction and parents of children who are alienated today would applaud if not that it does not change the article. 26 § 2 Civil Code. Despite many appeals and many information to Minister Ziobra, the government has not yet allowed itself any projects to amend this paragraph. I will read it because perhaps not everyone knows it, and it shows how paranoid Polish law can be, which regulates the lives of tens of thousands of children. Literally, if parental authority is equal to that of both parents residing in separate residences, the place of residence of the child is with the parent with whom the child is permanently resident. You understand. In this paragraph there is another sentence: if the child is not permanently staying with any of the parents - this is classic replacement care - his place of residence is determined by the custody court. Minister! This is a request from many backgrounds, mostly fatherly, but not only. Unfortunately, mothers are also alienated. Minister, if you don't change that, no change in the family and care code will be effective. I'm begging you to understand this. Thank you.
